Luna必答均分89.06%，Jev为88.02%，必答标签一致率93.90%。这些是裁判对同一批原回答的意见，不是裁判准确率或RAG回答正确率。未将两位裁判的分数平均，也未由整理者改判。
作答模型：deepseek-v4-flash；Embedding：BAAI/bge-m3；最多8段检索证据，相似度筛选阈值0.35。阈值不是答案正确概率。本轮只整理现有记录，不调用模型、不重新生成回答。
评委一：openai/gpt-6-luna；评委二：typesafe/jev-1.14-20261014。两者按相同v3规则、独立请求上下文评同32份答案；Jev复用已封存的匹配v3结果，未新增调用。

阅读方式

每题先看系统实际回答和参考答案，再看必答点和评语。陈述编号C1等仅在本题内有效，引用[1]等也是本题自己的映射；不同题目的[1]可能对应不同片段。R编号为跨题去重后的实际检索原文，Q编号为评委引用原文。HTML版可搜索题目和全文，展开详细证据，或打印。

评分规则

本轮采用 qa-scoring-v3，覆盖冻结v2题集中的部分规则表述。题目、参考答案、必答或可选属性、原文和数值容差保持冻结。以下说明以本轮v3为准。
要点分规则
0核心命题未回答、答错或与原文矛盾。相关背景或另一个要点不能代替本要点。
0.5至少一个实质部分正确，但缺条件、单位、计算或必要限制；不能含相反结论。复合要点未完整覆盖不等于全部错误。
1实质内容和必要限定均清楚表达。接受同义和明确等价表达，不要求照抄，但不替回答补上未表达的关系。
逐题必答要点得分 = 本题适用必答点分值之和 ÷ 适用必答点数 × 100%。总体均分 = 32道题得分的算术平均。不同题目等权，不是把所有要点直接相加后求平均。可选点仍展示，但省略不降低必答均分。
情形处理
只提供两个所需端点中的一个正确值及单位0.5分
分别定义峰强度法和峰面积法已区分测量口径，不必再写固定句式“不能混用”
独立要求说明相对光谱比例不等于绝对分子数量，但只解释比例定义该独立边界点为0分
要求指出来源冲突、限制或因果边界回答本身须表达该关系；仅列两个数不能自动补出冲突提醒
只给作用力不同，未表达归因限制0.5分；若明确以作用力不同作为替代解释且保留不确定性，可为1分
某指标是可选点漏答不扣必答均分；额外错误事实仍核查
维度规则和标签
科学事实支持全文、冻结标准证据和实际片段核对科学真伪。有支持、部分支持、无依据、矛盾；无依据不等于矛盾。
证据范围“当前检索没有”只检查当次全部检索或明确指定摘录；不能用全文中的数据补齐被截断的片段。
陈述分类纯可用性或能力声明为scope；含科学事实及范围限制的混合句为fact。范围说明不能免除科学事实检查。
单条引用该编号实际对应片段直接支持至少一个实质事实则1，否则0。不能借用另一段或全文补全它。
引用组仅合并该陈述实际附带的全部引用。complete为全部事实和必要限定获支持；partial为部分；missing为无实质支持。
范围陈述引用纯scope的事实引用分数和引用组为不适用，不当作0或满分；模型原生选择仍可单独显示。
数字检查同时核对浆种、转数、指标、单位、来源角色及题设绝对容差。只出现一个正确数字不等于答对。
推导边界计算须有全部输入。相关性不自动证明因果，也不能补出未给出的定量预测公式。
未测或缺失未评分、无适用分母、未返回费用等使用不适用或未知，不能替换成0。
共有维度达标要求：全部适用必答点为1、全部陈述有支持、每个适用的事实引用为1、每条事实陈述的引用组完整。不同维度不相加成一个总分，同一遗漏可关联多个维度但不重复累加扣分。
Luna另评作答边界，0为边界判断错误，1为部分正确但遗漏可答内容或未清楚区分未知，2为符合题目预期行为。它还给出检索覆盖、缺陷和关键错误；这些不是Jev已经完成的项目。Luna完整严格达标还要求边界为2且无关键错误，本文件对两裁判比较统一使用共有维度达标。
Jev原生接口输出选项、概率分布、置信度及证据定位，没有生成自由文字理由。下文的选项释义来自共同评分规则，不是补写的Jev评语。所选项概率和confidence是供应商诊断值，未经专家校准，不能解释为评分正确率。
Luna文字评语为模型返回的评审摘要；本文不声称展示模型隐藏推理。Jev对某条陈述选出的依据，不自动等同于它对某个必答要点的直接理由。

PFI2-001 这篇PFI打浆论文研究的是哪两类纸浆？原料是否经过漂白，采用什么制浆方法？

题型：事实；难度：简单；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

该研究使用的两类纸浆是商业漂白针叶木浆和漂白阔叶木浆。 [1]
原料经过漂白，制浆方法为硫酸盐法（kraft pulp）。 [1]

参考答案

研究对象为商业漂白针叶木硫酸盐浆和商业漂白阔叶木硫酸盐浆。方法部分没有给出具体树种名称。

PFI2-003 这篇研究为两类纸浆设置了哪些PFI打浆转数？每类有几个转数水平？

题型：事实；难度：简单；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

研究中漂白软木硫酸盐浆和漂白硬木硫酸盐浆分别采用 0、4000、8000、12000、16000 转的 PFI 打浆转数。 [1] [8]
每类纸浆有 5 个 PFI 打浆转数水平，其中包括未打浆的 0 转。 [1] [8]

参考答案

两类浆均设置0、4000、8000、12000和16000转，各5个转数水平。这里的转数水平数不等于独立重复实验数。

PFI2-004 PFI打到12000转的时候，针叶木浆和阔叶木浆的打浆度各是多少？那我们以后想要63 °SR左右的浆，是不是直接打12000转就行了？

题型：比较；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

该研究中PFI打浆转数设置为0、4000、8000、12000和16000转。 [1]
按文中对应关系，针叶木浆在12000转时打浆度为63 °SR。 [7]
按文中对应关系，阔叶木浆在12000转时打浆度为57 °SR。 [7]
打浆度会随PFI打浆转数增加而显著提高，并与纤维润胀和WRV增加有关。 [4]
本研究中12000转仅使针叶木浆达到63 °SR，而阔叶木浆为57 °SR，故不能直接把12000转套用于所有目标约63 °SR的浆。 [7]

参考答案

方法部分给出的12000转对应针叶木63 °SR、阔叶木57 °SR。相同转数在本文不同浆种/条件下得到的打浆度不同，不能据此建立适用于所有纸浆的唯一换算。两类浆的作用力也不同，不能把差异全部归因于浆种。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P112000转时针叶木63 °SR。是11
P212000转时阔叶木57 °SR。是11
P3相同转数不能在跨浆种、跨条件时保证相同打浆度；作用力也是不同的条件。是0.50.5

P1 评分依据

题设数值检查：针叶木打浆度 期望63 °SR 绝对容差±0
Luna 1分 原始评语：正确给出12000转时针叶木浆为63 °SR。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.98；置信度 0.97。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

P2 评分依据

题设数值检查：阔叶木打浆度 期望57 °SR 绝对容差±0
Luna 1分 原始评语：正确给出12000转时阔叶木浆为57 °SR。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.98；置信度 0.97。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

P3 评分依据

Luna 0.5分 原始评语：正确指出相同转数在两类浆中对应的打浆度不同，因此不能直接推广为所有目标约63 °SR纸浆的统一转数；但未提到针叶木与阔叶木采用的作用力也不同，边界条件说明不完整。
Jev 0.5分 部分覆盖；选择 partial；所选项概率 0.88；置信度 0.82。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

PFI2-006 WRV在本文中如何测定和计算？请解释公式中两个质量的含义。

题型：方法；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

WRV采用0.5 g绝干样品并按ISO 23714测定 [1]
WRV的计算公式为WRV(%)=((m1−m2)/m2)×100% [1]
公式中的m1是离心后样品的质量 [1]
公式中的m2是烘干后样品的质量 [1]

参考答案

按ISO 23714使用0.5 g绝干样品测定。WRV=(m1−m2)/m2×100%，m1为离心后样品质量，m2为烘干样品质量。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1标准ISO 23714，样品0.5 g（绝干）。是11
P2公式为(m1−m2)/m2×100%。是11
P3m1为离心后质量，m2为烘干质量。是11

PFI2-007 CI-XI与CI-XA有什么区别？请给出本文CI-XI的公式，并说明CI-XA依据什么计算。

题型：方法；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

CI-XI是通过比较XRD峰强度得到的结晶度指数，CI-XA是通过比较XRD峰面积得到的结晶度指数。 [1]
本文中两类CI分别来自峰强度法（CI-XI）和峰拟合/峰面积法（CI-XA）。 [7]
本文CI-XI的公式为 CI-XI = (I200 − Iam)/I200 × 100%，其中I200是2θ=22°至23°之间(200)晶面的最大衍射强度，Iam是2θ=18°至19°之间无定形部分的最小衍射强度。 [7]
CI-XA等于结晶峰面积占整个衍射图谱总面积的面积百分比。 [5]
文中CI-XI值显著高于CI-XA，这可能是因为CI-XA分析中对无定形贡献存在过拟合。 [2]

参考答案

CI-XI用衍射峰强度计算：(I200−Iam)/I200×100%。CI-XA用晶区峰面积占总衍射面积的百分比计算，来自峰拟合。两者采用不同口径。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1CI-XI的公式为(I200−Iam)/I200×100%，依据峰强度。是11
P2CI-XA为晶区峰面积/总衍射面积×100%，依据拟合峰面积。是11
P3区分强度法与面积法，不能将两列当作同一测量口径混用。是10

P1 评分依据

Luna 1分 原始评语：正确给出CI-XI公式，并说明I200和Iam所指的强度及各自对应的角度区间。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.95；置信度 0.92。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

P2 评分依据

Luna 1分 原始评语：正确说明CI-XA依据结晶峰面积占总衍射面积的百分比计算，并指出采用峰拟合。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.84；置信度 0.76。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

P3 评分依据

Luna 1分 原始评语：清楚区分CI-XI的峰强度口径与CI-XA的峰面积口径，没有混用两种测量方法。
Jev 0分 未覆盖或错误；选择 missing；所选项概率 0.39；置信度 0.09。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

PFI2-008 不打浆和打到16000转的时候，针叶木和阔叶木的CI-XI、CI-XA分别是多少？

题型：数值；难度：简单；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

针叶木不打浆（0转）时，CI-XI 为 73.23%，CI-XA 为 40.46%。 [1]
针叶木打到16000转时，CI-XI 为 69.82%，CI-XA 为 34.20%。 [1]
阔叶木不打浆（0转）时，CI-XI 为 75.55%，CI-XA 为 47.16%。 [1]
阔叶木打到16000转时，CI-XI 为 73.55%，CI-XA 为 38.01%。 [1]

参考答案

针叶木：0转CI-XI 73.23%、CI-XA 40.46%；16000转CI-XI 69.82%、CI-XA 34.20%。阔叶木：0转CI-XI 75.55%、CI-XA 47.16%；16000转CI-XI 73.55%、CI-XA 38.01%。

PFI2-009 阔叶木的CI-XA从不打浆到打到8000转降了多少个百分点？相对降了百分之几？把算式写出来。

题型：数值；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

阔叶木CI-XA在0转时为47.16%，在8000转时为42.35%。 [2]
下降百分点算式为47.16－42.35＝4.81个百分点。 [2]
相对降幅算式为(47.16－42.35)/47.16×100%≈10.20%。 [2]

参考答案

阔叶木CI-XA由0转47.16%降到8000转42.35%：绝对差47.16−42.35=4.81个百分点；相对降幅4.81/47.16×100%≈10.20%。论文正文只给出0→16000转的整体降幅19.4%，这里是按表中数值自行计算的中间区间。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1使用阔叶木CI-XA 0转47.16%和8000转42.35%作为输入。是11
P2绝对差4.81个百分点，展示相减。是11
P3相对降幅约10.20%，以0转47.16为分母。是11

P1 评分依据

题设数值检查：0转CI-XA 期望47.16 % 绝对容差±0.01；8000转CI-XA 期望42.35 % 绝对容差±0.01
Luna 1分 原始评语：正确给出阔叶木CI-XA的0转值47.16%和8000转值42.35%。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.97；置信度 0.95。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

P2 评分依据

PFI2-011 是不是打浆转数越高，纤维素结晶度就越低？

题型：比较；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

PFI打浆对纤维素结晶度的影响存在不同结论：有研究认为结晶度下降，也有研究认为结晶度上升。 [4]
还有研究报道结晶度随打浆程度先升高后降低，既往研究并未识别出明确趋势。 [5]
该研究指出，PFI打浆过程中晶粒尺寸、结晶度和各类氢键含量总体变化不大，PFI打浆对超分子结构破坏相对较小。 [3]

参考答案

不是单调下降。针叶木CI-XI从4000转70.42%升到8000转72.54%；阔叶木也从73.01%升到75.06%。0转到16000转总体下降，但过程中先降、回升、再降。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1指出总体下降与过程非单调可以同时成立。是0.50
P2至少给出一个正确反例：针叶木70.42→72.54%，或阔叶木73.01→75.06%，均对应4000→8000转。是00

P1 评分依据

Luna 0.5分 原始评语：提到既往研究存在先升后降的非单调变化，但没有指出本研究从0转到16000转总体下降，且过程中回升；未完整表达题目要求的总体趋势与非单调性并存。
Jev 0分 未覆盖或错误；选择 missing；所选项概率 0.78；置信度 0.67。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

P2 评分依据

Luna 0分 原始评语：未提供针叶木或阔叶木4000转至8000转的任一正确数值反例。
Jev 0分 未覆盖或错误；选择 missing；所选项概率 0.94；置信度 0.91。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

PFI2-012 本文CI-XI和CI-XA的大小关系如何？两种方法的结果相关吗？请分别给出两类浆的相关系数及显著性。

题型：比较；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

CI-XI 的值显著高于 CI-XA。 [2]
CI-XI 与 CI-XA 两种方法的结果对针叶浆和阔叶浆均显著相关。 [2]
针叶浆中两种方法的相关系数为 0.919（p < 0.05）。 [2]
阔叶浆中两种方法的相关系数为 0.925（p < 0.05）。 [2]

参考答案

CI-XI明显高于CI-XA。两者显著正相关：针叶木r=0.919，阔叶木r=0.925，均p<0.05。这一对相关系数对应两个结晶度指标，不能与氢键含量—CI的相关系数混用。

要点与评分

P2针叶木r=0.919，p<0.05。是11
P3阔叶木r=0.925，p<0.05。是11

PFI2-013 PFI打浆过程中结晶度为什么会先降、再升、再降？

题型：机理；难度：困难；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

软木纤维和硬木纤维的CI在PFI打浆中均呈相似变化：先下降、再上升，随后又下降。 [5]
结晶度的波动可能是因为PFI打浆的机械作用作用于纤维素的不同区域。 [5]
在打浆初期，结晶度下降表明机械处理可能主要作用于结晶区。 [5]
随后结晶度上升可解释为更大的力作用于无定形区，并在PFI打浆的机械和热作用下于干燥时可能发生共结晶。 [5]
之后的下降可能是结晶区被持续破坏所致。 [5]

参考答案

作者提出：早期可能主要作用于晶区使CI下降；随后对无定形区的作用以及干燥时可能的共结晶使CI回升；后期晶区继续破坏使CI再降。原文使用可能、可以解释等措辞，属于作者的机制解释，不是排除其他机制后的唯一因果证明。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1早期可能主要作用于晶区。是11
P2中期可能涉及无定形区以及干燥中的共结晶。是11
P3后期可能持续破坏晶区。是11
P4保留原文不确定性，不将解释写成唯一已证实机制。是11

P1 评分依据

Luna 1分 原始评语：准确说明打浆早期CI下降可能表明机械处理主要作用于结晶区，并保留了“可能”的限定。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.98；置信度 0.97。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

P2 评分依据

Luna 1分 原始评语：说明CI回升可能涉及更大作用力作用于无定形区，以及干燥时可能发生共结晶，必要条件均有表达。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.98；置信度 0.97。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

P3 评分依据

Luna 1分 原始评语：准确说明后期下降可能源于结晶区持续破坏，并保留不确定性。

PFI2-014 按照作者的解释，PFI打浆为什么会提高WRV，又如何与纤维间结合联系起来？

题型：机理；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

作者指出，随着PFI打浆转数增加，打浆度和WRV显著提高，原因是更高程度的细纤维化产生了新的表面和更多孔隙。 [1]
以WRV表征的润胀增加使纤维在横向上更柔顺，从而有助于提高纤维网络的纤维间结合强度。 [1]
纤维形态分析和保水值结果表明，细纤维化以及微细纤维之间的分离随打浆过程增加。 [1]
PFI打浆的主要作用包括内细纤维化（使纤维壁分层、松散和润胀）和外细纤维化等，从而提高纤维的可及性、润胀性、柔顺性和纤维间结合能力。 [3]
作者还总结，PFI打浆提高了细纤维化程度（尤其是内细纤维化）、纤维润胀和纸页抗张强度，而未降低纤维本身的抗张强度。 [2]

参考答案

打浆促进细纤维化，产生新的表面和更多孔隙，使WRV及纤维膨胀提高；纤维横向更柔顺，有助于改善纤维网络中的纤维间结合。这是本文提出的解释链条，不能据此给出每一步的独立因果效应大小。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1细纤维化增加新表面与孔隙，WRV提高。是11
P2膨胀提高使纤维横向更柔顺，促进纤维间结合。是11

PFI2-015 针叶木和阔叶木打浆后纤维长度的变化一样吗？为什么会有差别？

题型：比较；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

PFI打浆过程中，针叶木纤维和阔叶木纤维的纤维长度均呈下降趋势。 [1]
针叶木纤维的纤维长度变化显著，而阔叶木纤维的纤维长度相对针叶木无显著变化。 [1]
这种差别可能是因为阔叶木纤维打浆时施加的 refining pressure 较低。 [1]
PFI打浆中 forces per bar length 对针叶木为 3.33 N/mm，对阔叶木为 1.77 N/mm。 [6]

参考答案

正文文字报告针叶木纤维长度变化明显，阔叶木相对变化不显著；作者认为可能与阔叶木采用较低的打浆作用力有关。两类浆的作用力不同，因此不能把差异全部归因于浆种。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1正文文字报告针叶木纤维长度变化更明显，阔叶木相对不显著。是11
P2较低打浆作用力是作者对阔叶木差异提出的可能解释。是11
P3两类浆作用力不同，不能将差异全部归因于浆种。是0.50.5

PFI2-016 为什么本文认为纸张抗张性能提高，并不意味着单根纤维本身的强度也一定提高？零距抗张在这里有什么用？

题型：机理；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

本文认为纸张抗张强度的提高主要来自PFI精磨过程中细纤维化和细组分的增加。 [1]
因此，纸张抗张性能提高不能直接等同于单根纤维本身强度提高。 [1]
零距抗张指数通常用于表征纤维的拉伸强度。 [1]
本文零距抗张结果表明PFI精磨没有降低纤维强度，这与Kouko等的报道一致。 [1]

参考答案

作者将纸张抗张性能改善主要联系到细纤维化、细小纤维及纤维间结合。零距抗张指数用于表征纤维本身的抗张强度，结果表明本文PFI处理没有降低纤维强度。因此应区分纸页网络性能和纤维本身强度，不能只凭纸张变强推断单根纤维必然变强。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1纸页抗张的改善与细纤维化、细小纤维和结合有关。是0.51
P2零距抗张用于表征纤维强度，本文结论是没有降低纤维强度。是11
P3区分纸页与纤维层面的强度，不作必然同步提高推断。是11

P1 评分依据

Luna 0.5分 原始评语：正确指出纸页抗张强度提升主要与细纤维化和细组分增加有关，但必答点还要求说明纤维间结合，答案未提及。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.92；置信度 0.88。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

P2 评分依据

Luna 1分 原始评语：说明零距抗张用于表征纤维拉伸强度，并指出本文结果显示PFI精磨未降低纤维强度。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.94；置信度 0.91。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

P3 评分依据

Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.74；置信度 0.61。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

PFI2-017 本文中哪类浆的湿纸幅强度增幅更明显？作者如何解释？

题型：比较；难度：简单；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

与硬木纤维相比，软木纤维的湿纸幅强度（wet-web strength）增幅更明显。 [1]
作者解释这一现象是因为纤维长度在湿纸幅强度中起着关键作用。 [1]

参考答案

针叶木的湿纸幅强度增幅更明显。作者认为纤维长度在湿纸幅强度中起重要作用。

PFI2-018 这篇论文如何从FT-IR获得不同氢键的相对含量？请说明拟合波段、函数和含量口径。

题型：方法；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

论文对FT-IR羟基吸收带3000–3700 cm−1进行非线性拟合与解卷积。 [3] [6]
拟合前光谱在1016 cm−1归一化，并用Savitzky–Golay算法获得导数曲线；各氢键组分的波数取自二阶导数谱中对应归属区域，随后在PeakFit 4.12软件中用Gaussian函数进行非线性拟合。 [3]
该羟基带被解卷积为四个主要子带：Free OH(2) and OH(6)、O(6)H···O(3′)、O(3)H···O(5)、O(2)H···O(6)。 [6]
不同氢键的相对含量按对应子带的面积占比计算，即各子带面积占拟合总面积的比例。 [6]

参考答案

先归一化并用二阶导数辅助确定峰位；对3000–3700 cm⁻¹羟基区域采用高斯函数非线性拟合，软件为Peakfit 4.12；各组分相对含量按峰面积占比计算，不能当作绝对氢键数量。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1拟合区域3000–3700 cm⁻¹，采用高斯函数非线性拟合。是11
P2相对含量根据各组分峰面积占比计算。是11
P3相对峰面积比例不等于绝对氢键数量。是00

Luna 1分 原始评语：明确指出3000–3700 cm⁻¹羟基带采用Gaussian函数进行非线性拟合，符合拟合区域与函数要求。

PFI2-019 O(6)H···O(3′)、O(3)H···O(5)、O(2)H···O(6)这三种氢键的红外峰大概在多少波数？

题型：数值；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键的红外峰约在3227 cm−1。 [1]
文献给出的O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键范围约为3310–3230 cm−1。 [2]
O(3)H···O(5)分子内氢键的红外峰约在3346 cm−1。 [1]
文献给出的O(3)H···O(5)分子内氢键范围约为3375–3340 cm−1。 [2]
O(2)H···O(6)分子内氢键的红外峰约在3452 cm−1。 [1]
文献给出的O(2)H···O(6)分子内氢键范围约为3455–3410 cm−1。 [2]

参考答案

正文描述的约值依次为3227、3346、3452 cm⁻¹。O(6)H···O(3′)为分子间氢键，另外两类为分子内氢键；这些是正文约值。

PFI2-020 论文说打浆后氢键能量和距离基本不变，那是不是说明打浆对氢键网络基本没影响？

题型：机理；难度：困难；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

论文报告三种主要H键的峰位在打浆全过程中几乎不变，因此由公式得到的H键能量和H键距离基本不变。 [2]
但论文摘要同时指出，打浆后结晶指数、微晶尺寸和各类H键的含量发生了变化。 [4]
论文结论也把微晶尺寸、结晶度和各类H键含量列为PFI打浆作用的超分子结构对象。 [7]
论文进一步报告分子间O(6)H···O(3′) H键含量与结晶指数显著相关，说明H键网络与晶体堆积相关。 [5]
因此，能量和距离不变只能说明已识别H键的键能/键长参数稳定，不能推出氢键网络整体无影响；更准确是氢键数量/比例和晶体堆积发生变化。 [2] [4] [5]

参考答案

不表示完全不变。本文三类氢键的峰位几乎不变，因此据公式推得能量和距离基本不变；但相对含量仍变化：0到16000转，针叶木分子间氢键由28.23%变为23.56%，阔叶木由26.55%变为24.98%。应区分单类氢键能量/距离与不同组分的相对分布。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1峰位基本稳定，对应估算能量与距离基本不变。是11
P2指出至少一类氢键的相对含量随打浆变化；不强制给两类浆的数值例子。是11
P3区分能量/距离与组分含量，不能推断网络完全不变。是11
P4可选：为两类浆各给一个准确的含量变化例子。否 可选00

Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.96；置信度 0.94。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

PFI2-021 阔叶木的O(3)H···O(5)分子内氢键相对含量，从4000转到12000转变化了多少个百分点？相对变化是百分之几？

题型：数值；难度：中等；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

表2b列出阔叶木4000转时O(3)H···O(5)分子内氢键相对含量为33.06%。 [8]
所给表2b摘录只显示到8000转，未给出阔叶木12000转时O(3)H···O(5)的对应数值。 [8]
证据仅说明阔叶木O(3)H···O(5)在整个精磨过程中总体增加8.8%，不足以推算12000转的数值。 [2]

参考答案

由4000转33.06%降到12000转29.28%：减少33.06−29.28=3.78个百分点；相对降幅3.78/33.06×100%≈11.43%。含量是FT-IR拟合峰面积占比，不是绝对氢键数量；该区间结果为按表计算，正文未直接给出。

P3相对降幅约11.43%，以4000转33.06为分母。是00

PFI2-023 针叶木的O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键含量和CI-XI的Pearson相关系数是多少？

题型：原文冲突；难度：困难；预期行为：指出来源冲突。

系统实际回答

针叶木中O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键含量与CI-XI的Pearson相关系数为0.912（p<0.05）。 [1]
同一文献摘要中，针叶木的该Pearson相关系数写为0.888（p<0.05）。 [3]

参考答案

不一致：摘要给针叶木0.888，正文3.4节给0.912；两处阔叶木均为0.889，均称p<0.05。应分别说明位置并标记冲突，不能未经核实替作者选择一个数作为唯一正确值。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1摘要针叶木r=0.888，注明来自摘要。是11
P2正文3.4针叶木r=0.912，注明来自正文。是11
P3指出冲突，保留两个数及来源，不替作者无依据纠错。是10.5

P1 评分依据

题设数值检查：摘要针叶木r 期望0.888 dimensionless 绝对容差±0.0005
Luna 1分 原始评语：给出摘要中的针叶木相关系数0.888（p<0.05），并明确指出该值来自摘要。

PFI2-024 PFI打浆是怎么一步步让纸变结实的？纤维长度、细小纤维、WRV这几个因素里哪个贡献最大？

题型：整合；难度：困难；预期行为：正常回答。

系统实际回答

PFI精炼对纤维的主要作用包括内纤丝化（纤维壁分层、疏松、润胀）、外纤丝化（纤丝从纤维表面剥离但仍附着）、纤维缩短（剪切作用切断纤维）以及细小纤维生成。 [3]
这些PFI精炼作用会提高纤维的可及性、润胀、柔顺性和纤维间结合能力。 [3]
随着PFI精炼转数增加，打浆度和WRV显著上升，其原因是更高程度的纤丝化产生了新的表面和更多孔隙。 [2]
以WRV表征的润胀增加使纤维在横向更柔顺，从而有助于提高纤维网络的纤维间结合强度。 [2]
纤维形态分析显示外纤丝化随精炼过程增加，与比表面积增大相一致。 [5]
对针叶木纤维而言，宏观纤丝化指数和纤维长度随PFI精炼呈下降趋势，而细小纤维含量呈上升趋势。 [5]
抗张强度的提高主要归因于PFI精炼过程中不断增加的纤丝化和细小纤维。 [1]
零-span抗张指数结果表明PFI精炼并未降低纤维强度，因此纸页增强主要来自纤维间结合而非单根纤维强度下降。 [1]
湿纸幅强度在针叶木纤维中的提升比阔叶木更显著，因为纤维长度对湿纸幅强度起关键作用。 [1]
综合来看，纤丝化和细小纤维是抗张强度提升的主要贡献因素，纤维长度更多影响湿纸幅强度，而WRV/润胀主要通过提高纤维柔顺性和结合强度间接发挥作用。 [1] [2]

参考答案

本文将PFI打浆与细纤维化、孔隙/新表面和WRV增加联系起来，膨胀后的纤维更柔顺，结合能力改善，纸页抗张性能提高；零距结果表明纤维强度未降低。论文没有提供各纤维因素独立贡献的排名或控制其他因素后的效应大小，不能把这些定性解释直接变成因果贡献率。

PFI2-027 这篇论文对干纸抗张性能有什么结论？另外请给出同一批样品的耐破指数和撕裂指数精确数据。

题型：部分可答；难度：困难；预期行为：部分回答。

系统实际回答

论文指出PFI打浆提高了细纤维化程度（尤其是内部细纤维化）和纤维润胀，从而提高了干纸的抗张强度，且零距抗张指数表明纤维自身强度未下降。 [6]
提供的证据只报告了抗张指数、零距抗张指数和湿纸幅强度等相关结果，没有包含耐破指数或撕裂指数的精确数据。 [6]

参考答案

可回答部分：作者报告PFI打浆提高纸页抗张性能，并将其与细纤维化及细小纤维增加联系。无法回答部分：当前正文没有报告同批样品耐破指数和撕裂指数的精确数据，需要相应试验资料，不能由抗张结果直接换算。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev

P2明确说明耐破与撕裂精确数据缺失，不提供编造值或未经支持的换算。是11

P1 评分依据

Luna 0.5分 原始评语：正确说明抗张性能提高，并提及细纤维化和润胀；但未提到论文将抗张增幅主要归因于细纤维化和细小纤维增加，且将润胀也直接表述为原因，回答不完整。

PFI2-028 请比较这篇PFI论文与《Non-Wood Fibers: Relationships of Fiber Properties with Pulp Properties》的全部实验数据，给出两篇共同结论。

题型：证据不足；难度：困难；预期行为：说明不足并拒答。

系统实际回答

提供的证据文件均为 Li-2020-PFI-refining-aggregation-structure-cellulose.pdf，未包含《Non-Wood Fibers: Relationships of Fiber Properties with Pulp Properties》的正文、实验数据或结论。 [1] [7]
因此无法对两篇论文的全部实验数据进行逐项比较。 [1] [7]
在现有证据范围内，PFI 论文报告了 PFI 精磨对软木纤维和硬木纤维聚集结构及氢键网络的影响。 [7]
PFI 论文指出，在 PFI 精磨过程中，CI-XI 与 O(6)H···O(3′) 氢键含量在软木纤维和硬木纤维中均呈显著正相关。 [6]
由于缺少另一篇论文的实验数据，无法据此给出两篇论文的共同结论。 [1] [7]

PFI2-030 为什么阔叶木打浆后湿纸幅强度涨得比针叶木还多？

题型：错误前提；难度：中等；预期行为：纠正问题前提。

系统实际回答

现有证据表明，PFI打浆后针叶木纤维的湿纸幅强度增幅比阔叶木纤维更显著。 [2]
该差异归因于纤维长度在湿纸幅强度中起关键作用。 [2]
PFI打浆提高细纤维化（尤其内部细纤维化）、纤维润胀和纸页拉伸强度，且不降低纤维本身的拉伸强度。 [2]
打浆产生新表面和更多孔隙，WRV升高使纤维横向更柔顺，从而有助于改善纤维网络的纤维间结合强度。 [1]

参考答案

这个前提与论文不符：论文报告的是针叶木湿纸幅强度的增幅比阔叶木更明显，作者将其与纤维长度在湿纸幅强度中的重要作用联系起来。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1指出前提错误：增幅更明显的是针叶木，不是阔叶木。是11
P2给出作者解释：纤维长度对湿纸幅强度起重要作用。是11

P1 评分依据

Luna 1分 原始评语：明确指出针叶木湿纸幅强度增幅比阔叶木更显著，纠正了题目关于阔叶木增幅更大的错误前提。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.97；置信度 0.95。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

P2 评分依据

Luna 1分 原始评语：明确说明作者将湿纸幅强度差异与纤维长度的重要作用联系起来。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.99；置信度 0.98。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

PFI2-031 PFI打浆把纤维素从I型变成II型以后，结晶度为什么还会下降？

题型：错误前提；难度：中等；预期行为：纠正问题前提。

系统实际回答

XRD结果表明，PFI处理过程中软木和硬木纤维的纤维素晶型没有发生变化，说明PFI是相对温和的机械处理。 [8]
因此，所给证据不支持PFI打浆把纤维素从I型变成II型的前提。 [8]
尽管晶型未变，PFI打浆后CI仍可下降：软木纤维的CI-XI和CI-XA分别降低4.66%和15.47%，硬木纤维分别降低2.65%和19.4%。 [8]
CI随PFI打浆呈先降后升再降的波动，被归因于机械作用作用于纤维素不同区域。 [1]
第一阶段CI下降表明机械处理可能主要作用于结晶区。 [1]
随后CI上升可解释为更大作用力作用于无定形区，并在PFI机械和热作用下的干燥过程中可能发生共结晶。 [1]
后续CI下降则可能是结晶区持续被破坏。 [1]
PFI打浆会改变纤维的宏观结构和性质，这些变化被认为与打浆过程中结晶度和氢键网络的变化有关。 [4]
由于纤维素结晶区含有大量氢键，可假设PFI打浆过程中氢键网络会发生变化。 [2]

参考答案

前提不成立：论文指出两种纤维的纤维素晶型在PFI处理中没有变化（依据附件XRD图S1、S2），作者据此认为PFI打浆是较温和的机械处理。结晶度方面，0→16000转总体下降但过程有波动，例如针叶木CI-XI由73.23%到69.82%。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1指出论文报告晶型未改变，不存在I型→II型转变。是11
P2可选：继续说明结晶度整体下降但过程非单调。否 可选11

P1 评分依据

Luna 1分 原始评语：明确指出论文报告两种纤维在PFI处理过程中晶型没有变化，并据此否定I型转为II型的前提，完整回应必答点。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.95；置信度 0.92。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.88；置信度 0.82。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

PFI2-032 论文里氢键含量和CI-XI的相关系数是针叶木0.919、阔叶木0.925吧？这么高是不是就能用CI-XI直接算出氢键含量了？

题型：相关系数混淆；难度：困难；预期行为：纠正问题前提。

系统实际回答

0.919和0.925是CI-XI与CI-XA之间的相关系数，不是氢键含量与CI-XI的相关系数。 [6]
论文3.4节报告O(6)H···O(3′)氢键含量与CI-XI的Pearson相关系数为针叶木0.912、阔叶木0.889（p < 0.05）。 [2]
论文摘要给出的对应值为针叶木0.888、阔叶木0.889，其中针叶木数值与3.4节的0.912不一致。 [7] [2]
作者表述为O(6)H···O(3′)氢键含量的趋势可基于CI-XI在PFI精磨过程中进行推测，并未给出用CI-XI直接定量计算氢键含量的结论。 [5]
O(2)H···O(6)氢键或自由OH(2)、OH(6)与CI-XI的相关性差，O(3)H···O(5)氢键与CI-XI也没有相关性。 [4]

参考答案

不对。0.919/0.925是CI-XI与CI-XA两种结晶度之间的相关系数。O(6)H···O(3′)含量与CI-XI的相关系数：摘要写针叶木0.888、阔叶木0.889，正文3.4节写针叶木0.912、阔叶木0.889，针叶木两处不一致。作者只说可据CI-XI"大致推测"该氢键含量的变化趋势，这是5个转数水平上的相关，不是经过验证的定量换算模型。

要点与评分

要点要求是否计入必答分LunaJev
P1指出0.919/0.925属于CI-XI与CI-XA之间。是11
P2给出氢键—CI-XI的实际系数并指出摘要0.888与正文0.912不一致（阔叶木0.889）。是11
P3相关性只支持趋势推测，不等于可直接定量换算。是11

Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.99；置信度 0.98。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

Luna 1分 原始评语：给出摘要的针叶木0.888、阔叶木0.889，以及正文3.4节的针叶木0.912、阔叶木0.889，并指出针叶木两处不一致。
Jev 1分 完整覆盖；选择 complete；所选项概率 0.93；置信度 0.89。未返回该要点的自由文字理由。

2. Methods
2.1. PFI Reﬁning
Commercial bleached softwood and hardwood kraft pulp ﬁbers were obtained from
Zhongzhirenhe Company (Jinan, Shandong Province, China). Two pulp were treated with a PFI reﬁner
(Hamjern Maskin A/S, Hamar, Norway) according to Tappi T248 wd-97, with a pulp consistency
of 10%, the gap between the opposite reﬁning plates was 0.2 mm, and forces per bar length were
3.33 N/mm and 1.77 N/mm for softwood ﬁbers and hardwood ﬁbers, respectively. The drainability was
characterized by the Schopper Riegler number (SR-number) with the standards ISO 5267-1. Two pulp
ﬁbers were separately reﬁned in a PFI reﬁner at 0, 4000, 8000, 12,000, and 16,000 revolutions and
at 0, 4000, 8000, 12,000, and 16,000 revolutions, respectively. Corresponding various pulps with an
increasing beating degree, such as 16, 20, 40, 63, and 79◦

ns forskningsinstitut (PFI) reﬁning is the most common laboratory reﬁner in order to
improve the inter-ﬁber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-ﬁbrillated
or nano-ﬁbrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main eﬀects of PFI reﬁning on ﬁbers are: (i) internal
ﬁbrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of ﬁber wall; (ii) external ﬁbrillation—peeling of
ﬁbrils from the ﬁber surface while remaining attached; (iii) ﬁber shortening—ﬁber cutting due to
shearing action, and (iv) ﬁnes formation—primary ﬁnes come from ray cells and parenchyma cells
and microﬁbrils that are caused by ﬁber shortening or external ﬁbrillation. Moreover, the PFI reﬁning
could be adopted to obtain a higher internal ﬁbrillation than other laboratory reﬁners [1]. These eﬀects
of the PFI reﬁning on cellulosic ﬁbers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the ﬂexibility, and the
inter-ﬁber bonding capacity of ﬁbers. It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and
properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during reﬁning [7,8].

cellulos

In Figure 3, the tensile index, zero-span tensile index, and wet-web strength represented as a
function of the reﬁning revolutions. The increment of tensile strength is mainly caused by the increasing
ﬁbrillation and ﬁnes during PFI reﬁning, as mentioned above. The wet–web strength of softwood

characterizing the tensile strength of ﬁbers. The results of the zero-span tensile index showed that
PFI reﬁning did not reduce ﬁber strength, which is consistent with the report of Kouko et al. [24].
The above results showed that PFI reﬁning improved the extent of ﬁbrillation (especially the internal
ﬁbrillation), the swelling of ﬁbers, and the tensile strength of sheets without a decrease of the tensile
strength of ﬁbers.

Article
Probing Eﬀect of Papirindustriens Forskningsinstitut
(PFI) Reﬁning on Aggregation Structure of Cellulose:
Crystal Packing and Hydrogen-Bonding Network
Kunpeng Li, Lihong Zhao * and Beihai He
State Key Laboratory of Pulp and Paper Engineering, South China University of Technology, 381 Wushan Rd.,
Tianhe District, Guangzhou 510640, China; felikp@mail.scut.edu.cn (K.L.); ppebhhe@scut.edu.cn (B.H.)
* Correspondence: zhaolh@scut.edu.cn
Received: 17 November 2020; Accepted: 2 December 2020; Published: 4 December 2020
Abstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that aﬀects the properties of cellulosic
ﬁbers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) reﬁning on the
molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between
the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic ﬁbers. The results showed that
the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost
unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed
with reﬁning. Therein, the content of the inter-molecular O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds was s

3. Results and Discussions
3.1. The Morphological Analysis, Swelling, and Mechanical Properties
For softwood ﬁbers and hardwood ﬁbers, the macro-ﬁbrillation index and the ﬁber length had
a downward trend, and the ﬁnes content has an upward trend for the softwood ﬁbers during PFI
reﬁning, as can be seen in Figure 1. The macro-ﬁbrillation index and ﬁber length of softwood ﬁbers
changed signiﬁcantly. However, two parameters of hardwood ﬁbers had no signiﬁcant variations
relative to softwood ﬁbers. It might be because of the lower reﬁning pressure that was applied in
hardwood ﬁbers. The macro-ﬁbrillation index characterizes external macro-ﬁbrillation. The results of
ﬁber morphology analysis showed that external ﬁbrillation increased with the reﬁning process. It is
consistent with the increasing speciﬁc surface area (as shown in Table S1 in Online Resource).
Figure 1. The morphological analysis for the unreﬁned and reﬁned samples: (a) softwood ﬁbers and
(b) hardwood ﬁbers.

revolutions, as can be seen in Figure 2. It is caused by the higher ﬁbrillation that prod

decreasing ﬁrst and then increasing, along with a decline, as shown in Figure 4. The ﬂuctuation of the
CI was probably because of the mechanical action of PFI reﬁning on the diﬀerent regions of cellulose.
In the ﬁrst reﬁning stage, the decrease of the CI showed that mechanical treatment may mainly act in
the crystalline region. Subsequently, the growth of the CI could be interpreted as greater force acting
on the amorphous region in which cocrystallization might occur upon drying under the mechanical
and thermal action of PFI reﬁning. The subsequent decrease might be the continuous destruction of
crystalline region.

and 333333 [27]. Therein, 234432 arrangement was found to be more probable based on calculation [28].

The results indicated that the ﬁbrillation and properties of ﬁbers are related to the variation of the
supramolecular structure during PFI reﬁning. It is noticeable that there was a signiﬁcantly positive
correlation between the CI-XI and content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds during PFI reﬁning for softwood
ﬁbers and hardwood ﬁbers. Thus, the trend of the content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds might be generally
speculated based on CI-XI while reﬁning. Besides, the crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of
various H-bonds did not change much in general. It is assumed that PFI reﬁning is an excellent
mechanical treatment of ﬁbrillation with relatively little destruction on the supramolecular structure.

The beating degree and the WRV had a signiﬁcant increment with increasing PFI reﬁning
revolutions, as can be seen in Figure 2. It is caused by the higher ﬁbrillation that produces the
new surface and more pores (as shown in Table S1 in Online Resource). The increase in swelling
characterized by WRV makes the ﬁbers more compliant in transverse [1], which contributes to
improving the inter-ﬁber bonding strength of the ﬁber network. The results of ﬁber morphology
analysis and water retention value showing that the ﬁbrillation and the separation between ﬁbrils
increase with the reﬁning process.

tion), and then
it made these planes suﬀer more mechanical actions, so there was more adhesion (cocrystallization)
and cleavage of cellulose chains created for (110) plane during PFI reﬁning.
However, the crystallite size that was perpendicular to (200) lattice plane of two ﬁbers had an
upward trend during PFI reﬁning. It could be explained in terms of coalescence of chains or some
crystallographic stacking of adjacent microﬁbrils, where the (200) planes were aligned upon drying [30].
Additionally, it was probably due to the weaker cleavage eﬀect on (200) lattice planes that was hard for
water molecules to penetrate.
3.3. Eﬀect of PFI Reﬁning on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds
A mixture of inter-molecular and intra-molecular H-bonds is considered to be the cause for the
broadening of OH bands in the FT-IR spectra. Therefore, taking derivative of the OH bands can improve
the resolution of various H-bonds. Normally, 3580–3550 cm−1 is for free OH(2) and OH(6), 3455–3410 cm−1
is for O(2)H···O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds, 3375–3340 cm−1 is for O(3)H···O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds,
and 3310–3230 cm−1 is for O(6)H···O(3′) inter-molecular H-bonds in cellulose

2.4. XRD Analysis
The crystallinity of pretreated ﬁbers was determined by a Shimadzu XRD 7000S (Shimadzu,
Kyoto, Japan), while using Cu-Ka radiation. The samples were scanned at 40 kV and 30 mA in a 2θ
range of 4–40◦at 1◦/min. It is well known that two polymorphs, cellulose Iα and Iβ, are present in
native cellulose; for clarity, all of the reﬂections indexed here are on the Iβ lattice. Before analysis,
the experimental data is nine point smoothed with Savitzky–Golay algorithm. The two types of CI
values were obtained from peak intensity method (CI −XI) and peak ﬁtting (CI-XA), respectively.

Keywords: natural cellulosic ﬁbers; mechanical reﬁning; Crystallinity; Crystallite size;
Hydrogen bonds
1. Introduction
Mechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired
physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic ﬁbers. Therein,
Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) reﬁning is the most common laboratory reﬁner in order to
improve the inter-ﬁber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-ﬁbrillated
or nano-ﬁ
R021 Li-2020-PFI-refining-aggregation-structure-cellulose.pdf 第7至7页

4.1.2 software). The result is consistent with the report of Haigler & Roberts [26], which shows that
there might be two microﬁbrils associated along part of their length.
Figure 5. Cross-section model of cellulose crystallite for softwood ﬁbers and hardwood ﬁbers.
It is known that both the (110) and the (110) lattice plane are hydrophilic, and the (200)lattice
plane is hydrophobic in cellulose Iβ (as Figure 5 shows). In addition, it is noticeable that the (110)
planes possess the highest theoretical roughness and accessibility to the hydrophilic groups in cellulose
Iβ [28]. Throughout the reﬁning of two pulp ﬁbers, the sizes perpendicular to (110) plane were more
volatile than the (110) plane and (200) plane, which is consistent with the report of Duchemin et al.,
as shown in Table 1a,b [29]. It could be interpreted to be that water molecules were easier to permeate
through the spaces between the hydrophilic (110) planes in paracrystalline region (hydration), and then
it made these planes suﬀer more mechanical actions, so there was more adhesion (cocrystallization)
and cleavage of cellulose chains created for (110) plane during PFI reﬁning.

ntensity value of the (200) lattice plane between the scattering
angles of 2θ = 22◦and 23◦, and Iam is the minimum diﬀraction intensity of amorphous fraction between

All of the XRD diﬀractograms were smoothed with the Savitzky–Golay algorithm and
baseline-corrected with linear ﬁt function. Subsequently, the baseline-corrected diﬀractograms
were deconvoluted with the Pearson VII function. The value of the CI-XA equals the area percentage
occupied by crystalline peaks over the total area of the diﬀraction pattern [20]. In addition, the crystallite
size was calculated by the widely used Scherrer Equation (3) [21]:
k × λ
L = (3)
(β × cos θ)
where L is the size that is perpendicular to the lattice plane, k is a constant that depends on the crystal
shape (0.89), λ is the wavelength of the incident beam in the diﬀraction experiment (0.154 nm), β is the
full width at half maximum of the diﬀraction peak, and θ is the Bragg’s angle. In general, the values of
the calculated crystallite sizes might be lower than that of the real crystallite sizes, because the peak
broadening from the instrumental factors was not considered [22].

The H-bonding distance R is obtained while using the Pimentel and Sederholm equation [23],

△ν cm−1 = 4430 × (2.84 −R) (5)
△ν = νo −ν, νo is the monomeric OH stretching frequency, which is taken to be 3600 cm−1, ν is the

distance (Å).
The spectral was normalized at 1016 cm−1 and the derivative curve was obtained by applying
the Savitzky–Golay algorithm. The wavenumbers of H-bonding components are taken from
second-derivative spectra according to regions that are assigned to various H-bonds. Nonlinear ﬁtting
of the FT-IR spectral region from 3000 cm−1 to 3700 cm−1 was carried out with Gaussian function
in Peakﬁt 4.12 software (SeaSolve Software Inc., Framingham, MA, USA). The Pearson correlation
coeﬃcients were evaluated between the CI and content of various H-bonds, with a statistically
signiﬁcant p-value of <0.05, while using Spss 25.0 statistics software (IBM Corp., Armonk, NY, USA).

3.2. Eﬀect of PFI Reﬁning on Crystalline Index and Crystallite Size
The cellulose polymorph of these two ﬁbers had no change during PFI treatment, which illustrates
that the PFI reﬁning is a relatively mild mechanical treatment, as the results of XRD in Online Resource
(Figures S1 and S2) show. In general, the CI-XI and the CI-XA of softwood ﬁbers showed a reduction
of 4.66% and 15.47%, respectively, and those of hardwood ﬁbers decreased by 2.65% and 19.4%,
respectively, as can be seen in Figure 4 and Table 1. The value of the CI-XI was remarkably higher than
the CI-XA, which might be caused by the over-ﬁtting of amorphous contribution in the analysis of
CI-XA. However, the CI-XI and the CI-XA were correlated signiﬁcantly for both softwood ﬁbers and
hardwood ﬁbers, with a correlation coeﬃcient of 0.919 (p < 0.05) for softwood ﬁbers and 0.925 (p < 0.05)
for hardwood ﬁbers, respectively, which is consistent with

Figure 8. The trend of the CI-XI, the CI-XA, and the O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds: (a) softwood ﬁbers and
(b) hardwood ﬁbers.
Figure 9. The illustration of a two-phase structure of cellulose.
The correlation between the CI-XI and the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds or the free OH(2) and OH(6)
was poor for the following reasons. The free OH groups are easier for forming the O(2)H· · · O(6)
H-bonds as opposed to the O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds in the amorphous region, because the distance
between adjacent glucose units in a cellulosic molecular chain is closer than between the glucose units
in the adjacent molecular chain (as shown in Figure 9). Accordingly, when compared with the content
of O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds, there was less diﬀerence in the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds or the
free OH groups between the crystalline region and amorphous region, which sometimes made the
content of the two bonds not change much in regards to the crystallinity. The O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds
is stable, as mentioned above. It was presumed to change little as crystallinity changed during PFI
reﬁning. Hence, the correlation between O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds and the CI-XI was no

might disease; meanwhile, the content of the O(6)H···O(3′) or the O(2)H···O(6) H-bonds might increase
upon drying.
3.4. Correlation between CI and Content of Inter-Molecular H-Bonds during PFI Reﬁning
Similar to the CI-XI, the content of O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds decreased ﬁrst and then had an increase,
followed by a decline for the two ﬁbers (as shown in Figure 8a,b). Besides, the Pearson correlation
coeﬃcients were calculated between the content of H-bonds and the CI-XI. It was 0.912 (p < 0.05)
for softwood ﬁbers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood ﬁbers, respectively. The similar trends in the
content of the O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds and the CI-XI might be because that the crystalline region has a
higher content of O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds when compared with the amorphous regions, as illustrated
in Figure 9 [35].

As to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend

higher specific surface area (as shown in Figure S4 and Table S1 in Online Resource). This made cellulose
more accessible to water and chemicals. However, for the free OH(2) and OH(6) of hardwood fiber,

which makes it easy to form new H-bonds, such as O(2)H···O(6) or O(6)H···O(3′)H-bonds. Thus although
there is the formation of free OH(2) and OH(6) during fibrillation, the content of the free OH(2) and OH(6)

can be described as two phases. It had an increase of 3.5% from 31.4% to 32.5% in the stage of reﬁning
from 0 r to 4000 r and it then decreased by 21.57% from 4000 r to 12000 r, along with a growth of 15.26%
in the last reﬁning stage. For the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds of hardwood ﬁber, it increased
ﬁrstly and then had a decrease that was also followed by an increase. For both ﬁbers, the content of
O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds decreased, except for the stage of reﬁning from 0 r to 4000 r. It might be due to
the fact that the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds, which are much more accessible to water, are less stable than
O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds.
As to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend
in general. The enhanceme

2.5. FT-IR Analysis
THE FT-IR spectra were recorded on solid samples in KBr pellets by means of an FT-IR Bruker
Vertex 70 spectrometer (Karlsruhe, Germany). Unreﬁned and reﬁned ﬁbers were smashed into powder
between 40 and 60 mesh. Subsequently, the powder sample of each species (5 mg) was dispersed in a
matrix of highly pure KBr (500 mg). The spectral was recorded by averaging 64 recordings and at a
resolution of 4 cm−1 with transmission mode from 4000 cm−1 to 400 cm−1. The results were made on
the average spectrum that was obtained from ﬁve recordings.
The energy of the H-bonding EH for several OH stretching bands was calculated while using
Equation (4) [13].

νo is the wavenumber corresponding to free OH groups (3650 cm−1), ν is the frequency (cm−1) of

se [14]. Moreover, it was used
to study the diﬀerences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal
region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during
PFI reﬁning, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of
eucalyptus ﬁbers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that
the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3′) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions
of the content of certain H-bonds diﬀered at various reﬁning stages.
There is still a lack of literature on the eﬀects of PFI reﬁnement on the H-bonding network.
Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of
further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of
H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, ﬂexibility,
and ﬁbrillation of cellulosic ﬁbers during PFI reﬁnement. Therefore, this work explored the eﬀects
of PFI reﬁnement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of
H-

· O(5) H-bonds
is stable, as mentioned above. It was presumed to change little as crystallinity changed during PFI
reﬁning. Hence, the correlation between O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds and the CI-XI was not discussed in the
present study.
4. Conclusions
PFI reﬁning acts not only on the macro-level structure and mechanical properties, but also on
the supramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.

2. Methods
2.1. PFI Reﬁning
Commercial bleached softwood and hardwood kraft pulp ﬁbers were obtained from
Zhongzhirenhe Company (Jinan, Shandong Province, China). Two pulp were treated with a PFI reﬁner
(Hamjern Maskin A/S, Hamar, Norway) according to Tappi T248 wd-97, with a pulp consistency
of 10%, the gap between the opposite reﬁning plates was 0.2 mm, and forces per bar length were
3.33 N/mm and 1.77 N/mm for softwood ﬁbers and hardwood ﬁbers, respectively. The drainability was
characterized by the Schopper Riegler number (SR-number) with the standards ISO 5267-1. Two pulp
ﬁbers were separately reﬁned in a PFI reﬁner at 0, 4000, 8000, 12,000, and 16,000 revolutions and
at 0, 4000, 8000, 12,000, and 16,000 revolutions, respectively. Corresponding various pulps with an
increasing beating degree, such as 16, 20, 40, 63, and 79◦

2.2. Morphological Analysis of Fibers and Mechanical Properties of Sheets
The ﬁnes content, weighted mean length, and macro-ﬁbrillation index of each sample were
determined with a MORFI LB analyzer (Techpap SAS, Grenoble, France). The tensile strength of
dry sheets and Wet-web strength were tested according to TAPPI T494 and Chinese standard GBT
12914-2008, respectively. The zero-span tensile strength was tested with the Z-Span 2400 apparatus
(Pulmac International Co. Ltd., Montpelier, VT, USA), according to the standard ISO 15361.

The beating degree and the WRV had a signiﬁcant increment with increasing PFI reﬁning
revolutions, as can be seen in Figure 2. It is caused by the higher ﬁbrillation that produces the

characterized by WRV makes the ﬁbers more compliant in transverse [1], which contributes to
improving the inter-ﬁber bonding strength of the ﬁber network. The results of ﬁber morphology
analysis and water retention value showing that the ﬁbrillation and the separation between ﬁbrils
increase with the reﬁning process.

2.3. Water Retention Value (WRV)
The WRV was determined with 0.5 g samples (o.d.) according to ISO 23714.
(m1 −m2) WRV(%) = × 100% (1)

where m1 is the weight of the sample after centrifugation and m2 is the weight of the oven-dried sample.
2.4. XRD Analysis
The crystallinity of pretreated ﬁbers was determined by a Shimadzu XRD 7000S (Shimadzu,
Kyoto, Japan), while using Cu-Ka radiation. The samples were scanned at 40 kV and 30 mA in a 2θ
range of 4–40◦at 1◦/min. It is well known that two polymorphs, cellulose Iα and Iβ, are present in
native cellulose; for clarity, all of the reﬂections indexed here are on the Iβ lattice. Before analysis,
the experimental data is nine point smoothed with Savitzky–Golay algorithm. The two types of CI
values were obtained from peak intensity method (CI −XI) and peak ﬁtting (CI-XA), respectively.
The CI −XI was calculated as in Equation (2) [19].
(I200 −Iam) CI −XI = × 100% (2)
I200
where I200 is the maximum diﬀraction intensity value of the (200) lattice plane between the scattering
angles of 2θ = 22◦and 23◦, and Iam is the minimum diﬀraction intensity of amorphous fraction between
2θ = 18◦and 19◦.

All of the XRD diﬀractograms were smoothed with the Savitzky–Golay algorithm and
baseline-corrected with linear ﬁt function. Subsequently, the baseline-corrected diﬀractograms
were deconvoluted with the Pearson VII function. The value of the CI-XA equals the area percentage
occupied by crystalline peaks over the total area of the diﬀraction pattern [20]. In addition, the crystallite
size was calculated by the widely used Scherrer Equation (3) [21]:
k × λ
L = (3)
(β × cos θ)
where L is the size that is perpendicular to the lattice plane, k is a constant that depends on the crystal
shape (0.89), λ is the wavelength of the incident beam in the diﬀraction experiment (0.154 nm), β is the
full width at half maximum of the diﬀraction peak, and θ is the Bragg’s angle. In general, the values of
the calculated crystallite sizes might be lower than that of the real crystallite sizes, because the peak
broadening from the instrumental factors was not considered [22].

3.2. Eﬀect of PFI Reﬁning on Crystalline Index and Crystallite Size
The cellulose polymorph of these two ﬁbers had no change during PFI treatment, which illustrates
that the PFI reﬁning is a relatively mild mechanical treatment, as the results of XRD in Online Resource
(Figures S1 and S2) show. In general, the CI-XI and the CI-XA of softwood ﬁbers showed a reduction
of 4.66% and 15.47%, respectively, and those of hardwood ﬁbers decreased by 2.65% and 19.4%,
respectively, as can be seen in Figure 4 and Table 1. The value of the CI-XI was remarkably higher than
the CI-XA, which might be caused by the over-ﬁtting of amorphous contribution in the analysis of
CI-XA. However, the CI-XI and the CI-XA were correlated signiﬁcantly for both softwood ﬁbers and
hardwood ﬁbers, with a correlation coeﬃcient of 0.919 (p < 0.05) for softwood ﬁbers and 0.925 (p < 0.05)
for hardwood ﬁbers, respectively, which is consistent with

3.3. Eﬀect of PFI Reﬁning on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds
A mixture of inter-molecular and intra-molecular H-bonds is considered to be the cause for the
broadening of OH bands in the FT-IR spectra. Therefore, taking derivative of the OH bands can improve
the resolution of various H-bonds. Normally, 3580–3550 cm−1 is for free OH(2) and OH(6), 3455–3410 cm−1
is for O(2)H···O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds, 3375–3340 cm−1 is for O(3)H···O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds,
and 3310–3230 cm−1 is for O(6)H···O(3′) inter-molecular H-bonds in cellulose

Polymers 2020, 12, 2912 12 of 14
The results indicated that the ﬁbrillation and properties of ﬁbers are related to the variation of the
supramolecular structure during PFI reﬁning. It is noticeable that there was a signiﬁcantly positive
correlation between the CI-XI and content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds during PFI reﬁning for softwood
ﬁbers and hardwood ﬁbers. Thus, the trend of the content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds might be generally
speculated based on CI-XI while reﬁning. Besides, the crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of
various H-bonds did not change much in general. It is assumed that PFI reﬁning is an excellent
mechanical treatment of ﬁbrillation with relatively little destruction on the supramolecular structure.

Q065 评委原文依据

ns forskningsinstitut (PFI) reﬁning is the most common laboratory reﬁner in order to
improve the inter-ﬁber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-ﬁbrillated
or nano-ﬁbrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main eﬀects of PFI reﬁning on ﬁbers are: (i) internal
ﬁbrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of ﬁber wall; (ii) external ﬁbrillation—peeling of
ﬁbrils from the ﬁber surface while remaining attached; (iii) ﬁber shortening—ﬁber cutting due to
shearing action, and (iv) ﬁnes formation—primary ﬁnes come from ray cells and parenchyma cells
and microﬁbrils that are caused by ﬁber shortening or external ﬁbrillation. Moreover, the PFI reﬁning
could be adopted to obtain a higher internal ﬁbrillation than other laboratory reﬁners [1]. These eﬀects
of the PFI reﬁning on cellulosic ﬁbers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the ﬂexibility, and the
inter-ﬁber bonding capacity of ﬁbers. It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and
properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during reﬁning [7,8].
There are some diﬀerent conclusions regarding to the eﬀects of PFI reﬁning on crystallinity of
cellulos
Q066 当次检索 Li-2020-PFI-refining-aggregation-structure-cellulose.pdf 第5至5页

3. Results and Discussions
3.1. The Morphological Analysis, Swelling, and Mechanical Properties
For softwood ﬁbers and hardwood ﬁbers, the macro-ﬁbrillation index and the ﬁber length had
a downward trend, and the ﬁnes content has an upward trend for the softwood ﬁbers during PFI
reﬁning, as can be seen in Figure 1. The macro-ﬁbrillation index and ﬁber length of softwood ﬁbers
changed signiﬁcantly. However, two parameters of hardwood ﬁbers had no signiﬁcant variations
relative to softwood ﬁbers. It might be because of the lower reﬁning pressure that was applied in
hardwood ﬁbers. The macro-ﬁbrillation index characterizes external macro-ﬁbrillation. The results of
ﬁber morphology analysis showed that external ﬁbrillation increased with the reﬁning process. It is
consistent with the increasing speciﬁc surface area (as shown in Table S1 in Online Resource).

In Figure 3, the tensile index, zero-span tensile index, and wet-web strength represented as a function of the refining revolutions. The increment of tensile strength is mainly caused by the increasing fibrillation and fines during PFI refining, as mentioned above. The wet–web strength of softwood fiber had a more significant increase when compared to that of hardwood fiber. It is for that fiber length plays an essential role in wet–web strength. The zero-span tensile index was usually used in characterizing the tensile strength of fibers. The results of the zero-span tensile index showed that PFI refining did not reduce fiber strength, which is consistent with the report of Kouko et al. [24]. The above results showed that PFI refining improved the extent of fibrillation (especially the internal fibrillation), the swelling of fibers, and the tensile strength of sheets without a decrease of the tensile strength of fibers.

Polymers 2020, 12, 2912 4 of 14
The H-bonding distance R is obtained while using the Pimentel and Sederholm equation [23],
as follows.
△ν cm−1 = 4430 × (2.84 −R) (5)
△ν = νo −ν, νo is the monomeric OH stretching frequency, which is taken to be 3600 cm−1, ν is the
stretching frequency (cm−1) observed in the infrared spectrum of the sample, and R is the H-bonding
distance (Å).
The spectral was normalized at 1016 cm−1 and the derivative curve was obtained by applying
the Savitzky–Golay algorithm. The wavenumbers of H-bonding components are taken from
second-derivative spectra according to regions that are assigned to various H-bonds. Nonlinear ﬁtting
of the FT-IR spectral region from 3000 cm−1 to 3700 cm−1 was carried out with Gaussian function
in Peakﬁt 4.12 software (SeaSolve Software Inc., Framingham, MA, USA). The Pearson correlation
coeﬃcients were evaluated between the CI and content of various H-bonds, with a statistically
signiﬁcant p-value of <0.05, while using Spss 25.0 statistics software (IBM Corp., Armonk, NY, USA).

Probing Eﬀect of Papirindustriens Forskningsinstitut
(PFI) Reﬁning on Aggregation Structure of Cellulose:

Kunpeng Li, Lihong Zhao * and Beihai He
State Key Laboratory of Pulp and Paper Engineering, South China University of Technology, 381 Wushan Rd.,

Abstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that aﬀects the properties of cellulosic
ﬁbers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) reﬁning on the

PFI reﬁning acts not only on the macro-level structure and mechanical properties, but also on
the supramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.

1. Introduction
Mechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired
physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic ﬁbers. Therein,
Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) reﬁning is the most common laboratory reﬁner in order to
improve the inter-ﬁber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-ﬁbrillated
or nano-ﬁ
Q105 核验全文 doc_0fcf2a50cfa02d21

Q105 核验全文 doc_0fcf2a50cfa02d21

Q105 评委原文依据

This article is an open access article distributed under the terms and conditions of the Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY) license ( http://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/ ). Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that affects the properties of cellulosic fibers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining.

the formation of O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds. Both the fewer inter-molecular H-bonds and the lower CI
(as Section 3.2 shows) will further improve the ﬂexibility of cellulosic ﬁbers, due to the higher mobility
of molecular chains, the accessibility, and the WRV of ﬁbers (as seen in Section 3.1).
As to the O(3)H· · · O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds of softwood ﬁber, the content had a growth
of 10.09% from 28.44% to 31.31% in the stage of reﬁning from 0 r to 4000 r, and then had a slight
decrease of 1.44% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by an increase of 13.16% until the end of reﬁning.
Overall, the trend was similar to that of hardwood ﬁbers, which ﬁrst increased and then had a
decrease, followed by a growth. During the whole reﬁning, the amount of O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds for
softwood ﬁbers and hardwood ﬁbers showed an upward tendency and increased by 22.78% and 8.8%,
respectively. The increase might be because the stable O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds might have not only
less breakage under the action of water as destructor to the crystallites [30], but also creation in the
amorphous region upon drying. Because hydrogen bond–covalent bond synergies play an import

upon drying.
3.4. Correlation between CI and Content of Inter-Molecular H-Bonds during PFI Reﬁning
Similar to the CI-XI, the content of O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds decreased ﬁrst and then had an increase,
followed by a decline for the two ﬁbers (as shown in Figure 8a,b). Besides, the Pearson correlation
coeﬃcients were calculated between the content of H-bonds and the CI-XI. It was 0.912 (p < 0.05)
for softwood ﬁbers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood ﬁbers, respectively. The similar trends in the
content of the O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds and the CI-XI might be because that the crystalline region has a
higher content of O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds when compared with the amorphous regions, as illustrated
in Figure 9 [35].

Polymers 2020, 12, 2912 11 of 14
Figure 8. The trend of the CI-XI, the CI-XA, and the O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds: (a) softwood ﬁbers and
(b) hardwood ﬁbers.
Figure 9. The illustration of a two-phase structure of cellulose.
The correlation between the CI-XI and the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds or the free OH(2) and OH(6)
was poor for the following reasons. The free OH groups are easier for forming the O(2)H· · · O(6)
H-bonds as opposed to the O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds in the amorphous region, because the distance
between adjacent glucose units in a cellulosic molecular chain is closer than between the glucose units
in the adjacent molecular chain (as shown in Figure 9). Accordingly, when compared with the content
of O(6)H· · · O(3′) H-bonds, there was less diﬀerence in the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds or the
free OH groups between the crystalline region and amorphous region, which sometimes made the
content of the two bonds not change much in regards to the crystallinity. The O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds
is stable, as mentioned above. It was presumed to change little as crystallinity changed during PFI
reﬁning. Hence, the correlation between O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds and the CI-XI was no
